# Consideration of funding proposals - Addendum X

## Funding proposal package for FP262

### Summary

This addendum contains the following six parts:

1. **a) No-objection letter(s) issued by the national designated authority(ies) or focal point(s).**
2. **b) Environmental and Social report(s) disclosure.**
3. **c) Independent Technical Advisory Panel's assessment.**
4. **d) Response from the accredited entity to the independent Technical Advisory Panel's assessment.**
5. **e) Gender documentation of the funding proposal.**
6. **f) Summary.**

### Additional Information

This document is prepared and submitted for consideration by the GCF Board at a board meeting. It is published as part of the GCF website, which is not intended to constitute any opinion or recommendation by the GCF or its members. The official version of this document and opinions in it are governed by applicable GCF policies.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal V 3.0 | Page 8

## Contents

### Section A: Project / Programme Summary
- Brief overview of the project or programme
- Key objectives and expected outcomes

### Section B: Project / Programme Information
- Detailed description of the project or programme
- Scope, duration, and budget
- Stakeholders involved

### Section C: Financing Information
- Funding sources and amounts
- Repayment terms and conditions
- Risk management strategies

### Section D: Expected Performance Against Investment Criteria
- Metrics for evaluating project success
- Comparison with baseline data or previous projects

### Section E: Logical Framework
- A logical structure outlining the project's components and their interrelationships
- Assumptions, constraints, and potential risks

### Section F: Risk Assessment and Management
- Identification of potential risks and vulnerabilities
- Strategies for mitigating or managing these risks
- Contingency plans in case of adverse events

### Section G: GCF Policies and Standards
- Compliance with Green Climate Fund policies and standards
- Ethical considerations and transparency requirements

### Section H: Annexes
- Additional information, data, or supporting documents
- Definitions of key terms and acronyms

## Note to Accredited Entities on the use of the funding proposal template

* Accredited Entities are required to adhere to the following guidelines when using this template:
	+ Use only the approved fonts (Arial, size 11) for all text.
	+ Do not exceed 60 lines per page, including annexes.
	+ Proposals exceeding the prescribed length will not be assessed within the usual timeframe.
	+ The recommended font is Arial, size 11.
	+ All proposals must be submitted on the GCF website by the submission deadline.
	+ Annexes are subject to the redaction of any information that may not be disclosed pursuant to the IOP.
	+ Accredited Entities are required to submit their completed proposal to: [insert contact information]

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal V3.0 | Page 4

## A

La Banque Agricole (LBA) will support the small and medium holder farmers collaborating closely with Farmer Cooperatives (FCs), Cooperatives, Service providers/providers of services and Micro-, Small- and Medium Enterprises (MSMEs) to facilitate concessional loans aimed at promoting climate-smart agricultural practices. The LBA will also support the development of a digital lending platform that will improve the accessibility of concessional loans for farmers and MSMEs. Additionally, a GSA digital lending platform will improve the accessibility of concessional loans for farmers and MSMEs. Technical assistance will support Programme Management Unit (PMU) in implementing the project. The PMU will oversee the day-to-day implementation of the project and ensure that it is aligned with the objectives of the LBA.

The Programme Management Unit (PMU) will also be responsible for documenting successes, lessons learned, and best practices in agricultural value chain. The PMU will also be responsible for monitoring and evaluating the impact of the project on farmers and MSMEs.

The Programme Management Unit (PMU) will also be responsible for building resilience of approximately 152,266 direct and 535,147 indirect beneficiaries. The PMU will also be responsible for ensuring that the project is aligned with the objectives of the LBA.

# Green Climate Fund FUNDING PROPOSAL V3.0 | PAGE 9

## Figure 1: Average annual natural hazard occurrence for 1980-2020**

![Figure 1](https://i.imgur.com/7Z6jZ8M.png)

## 16. Senegal faces a range of natural hazards and risks, particularly flooding, droughts, wildfires, water scarcity, and climate change impacts. The country is vulnerable to these hazards due to its geographical location in the Sahel region, which is prone to extreme weather events.

## 17. Livestock production is crucial for feeding livestock. Climate change has significant negative impacts on livestock farming. Studies using process modeling and climate projections have shown that Senegal is particularly vulnerable due to its climate change vulnerability. This vulnerability is particularly pronounced in the Groundndi Basin, where the majority of farming (~47%) relies on named agriculture (1). For instance, groundnut yields in Senegal are projected to decrease by 25% by 2030 due to climate change. Additionally, the country is also vulnerable to droughts and floods, which can have devastating effects on livestock production and food security.

## 18. The agricultural system in Senegal is heterogeneously embedded in local food systems. It consists of the pastoral system (northern Senegal), the agropastoral system (eastern and southern Senegal) and the mixed system (central Senegal). The availability to the livestock production is especially high in the northern and eastern parts of the country, but it has been decreasing in the past few years. This decrease is due to frequent droughts and floods.

102. The Green Climate Finance Facility for Fostering Climate-Smart Agriculture in Senegal aims to significantly mitigate GHG emissions through a variety of targeted strategies.

107. As a cross-cutting Programme in Senegal, the aims, the Green Climate Finance Facility for Forestry (GCF) promotes climate-smart practices such as drip irrigation, climate-resilient seeds, and agroforestry to help farmers adapt to climate risks like droughts and floods. Through concessional loans and technical assistance, the Programme supports the development of climate-resilient agricultural practices in Senegal.

112. Potential for Knowledge Sharing and Learning: Knowledge sharing and learning are central to this programme for driving a gender-responsive climate change adaptation strategy. This programme shares knowledge through extensive training on climate-smart practices, financial literacy, and climate risk assessments. This approach not only empowers farmers and MSMEs with the knowledge needed to implement CSA strategies but also enhances their capacity to manage risks associated with climate change. The programme will share best practices and success stories facilitating learning among participants. 17. The Programme focuses on promoting climate-smart agricultural (CSA) practices that deliver significant co-benefits to soil health and the environment. CSA techniques such as zero tillage, crop rotation, and agroforestry are used to maintain soil structure and prevent erosion, thereby enhancing biodiversity within ecosystems. Practices like drip irrigation and mulching improve water use efficiency, particularly in water-scarce regions. Additionally, the Programme includes initiatives for rehabilitating degraded lands through reforestation and the adoption of conservation tillage. The Programme also focuses on improving soil health by increasing organic matter content and reducing nutrient losses through proper fertilizer management.

18. The Programme's social benefits extend to health, education, and community well-being. By reducing reliance on chemical fertilizers and pesticides, the Programme decreases exposure to harmful chemicals for both people and the environment. This reduces the incidence of diseases and promotes better health outcomes. The use of biogas for cooking reduces indoor air pollution, improving respiratory health. The Programme also contributes to dietary variety and food security by promoting the cultivation of diverse crops.

121. Agriculture serves as the primary livelihood for over 70% of Senegal’s population, relying heavily on rainfall and soil fertility to produce food. These vulnerabilities are exacerbated by climate change, which is projected to increase the frequency and severity of droughts in the country. The COVID-19 pandemic has also had a significant impact on the agricultural sector, with many farmers facing financial difficulties due to reduced demand for their products.

To address these challenges, the Green Climate Fund (GCF) is proposing a funding proposal that aims to support Senegal's efforts to enhance climate resilience and mitigate greenhouse gas emissions. The proposal includes several key initiatives:

1. Agricultural Adaptation and Resilience Programme (AARP): This programme will provide technical assistance and capacity building to farmers, helping them to adapt their practices to the impacts of climate change. It will also support the development of early warning systems for extreme weather events, such as droughts and floods.
2. Sustainable Agriculture Programme (SAP): This programme will promote sustainable agricultural practices, such as agroforestry and conservation agriculture, to help farmers to adapt to the impacts of climate change while also improving their productivity and profitability.
3. Climate-Smart Agriculture Programme (CSAP): This programme will support the development of climate-smart agricultural technologies and practices, such as precision agriculture and biofertilizers, to help farmers to increase their yields while reducing their greenhouse gas emissions.
4. Climate Change Mitigation Programme (CCMP): This programme will support the development of climate change mitigation projects in Senegal, such as renewable energy projects and reforestation efforts, to help reduce the country's greenhouse gas emissions.
5. Early Warning Systems for Extreme Weather Events: The proposal includes a plan to develop early warning systems for extreme weather events, such as droughts and floods, to help farmers to prepare for and respond to these events.

The Green Climate Fund is proposing that the GCF country commitment be increased from 1% of its GDP to 2% of its GDP in order to support Senegal's efforts to enhance climate resilience and mitigate greenhouse gas emissions. The proposal also includes a plan for regular reporting on the progress of the funding proposal, including an annual report on the impact of the funding on the country's climate change adaptation and mitigation efforts.

In conclusion, the Green Climate Fund is proposing a funding proposal that aims to support Senegal's efforts to enhance climate resilience and mitigate greenhouse gas emissions. The proposal includes several key initiatives, such as the Agricultural Adaptation and Resilience Programme (AARP), the Sustainable Agriculture Programme (SAP), the Climate-Smart Agriculture Programme (CSAP), the Climate Change Mitigation Programme (CCMP), and the Early Warning Systems for Extreme Weather Events. The proposal also includes a plan for increasing the GCF country commitment to 2% of its GDP in order to support Senegal's efforts to enhance climate resilience and mitigate greenhouse gas emissions.

14.5. The Monitoring, Reporting, and Verification (MRV) system for the Programme will be designed to measure both mitigation and adaptation results. # Green Climate Funding Proposal V 3.0 | Page 89

## Table of Contents

1. Introduction
2. Project Overview
3. Objectives and Scope
4. Methodology
5. Budget and Financial Projections
6. Risk Management
7. Monitoring and Evaluation
8. Conclusion
9. Appendices

## 1. Introduction

The Green Climate Funding Proposal V 3.0 is a comprehensive document outlining the proposed funding for climate change mitigation and adaptation projects in developing countries. This proposal aims to provide a clear and concise overview of the project's objectives, methodology, budget, and risk management strategies.

## 2. Project Overview

The Green Climate Funding Proposal V 3.0 focuses on supporting climate change mitigation and adaptation projects in developing countries. The proposed funding will be allocated to various sectors, including agriculture, energy, water, and forestry, among others. The project aims to reduce greenhouse gas emissions, enhance resilience to climate change impacts, and promote sustainable development.

## 3. Objectives and Scope

The primary objectives of the Green Climate Funding Proposal V 3.0 are:

1. To support climate change mitigation and adaptation projects in developing countries.
2. To enhance resilience to climate change impacts.
3. To promote sustainable development.
4. To reduce greenhouse gas emissions.
5. To support the transition to a low-carbon economy.

The scope of the Green Climate Funding Proposal V 3.0 includes various sectors, including agriculture, energy, water, and forestry, among others. The proposed funding will be allocated to support projects that align with these sectors and contribute to the achievement of the project's objectives.

## 4. Methodology

The methodology for the Green Climate Funding Proposal V 3.0 involves a comprehensive assessment of the potential impacts of climate change on developing countries. The proposed funding will be allocated based on the following criteria:

1. Project alignment with the project's objectives and scope.
2. Potential impacts of climate change on the project's target sectors.
3. Feasibility and sustainability of the project.
4. Alignment with the Green Climate Fund's principles and guidelines.
5. Potential for scalability and replicability.

## 5. Budget and Financial Projections

The budget for the Green Climate Funding Proposal V 3.0 is $10 billion over a period of five years (2026-2030). The proposed funding will be allocated to support climate change mitigation and adaptation projects in developing countries. The budget includes the following components:

1. Project implementation costs.
2. Monitoring and evaluation costs.
3. Administrative costs.
4. Contingency funds.

The financial projections for the Green Climate Funding Proposal V 3.0 are based on a conservative scenario, assuming an average annual growth rate of 5% in project implementation costs and a moderate growth rate in monitoring and evaluation costs. The proposed funding will be allocated to support projects that align with the project's objectives and scope.

## 6. Risk Management

The risk management strategy for the Green Climate Funding Proposal V 3.0 involves the following components:

1. Project selection and implementation: The proposed funding will be allocated to support projects that are aligned with the project's objectives and scope. The proposed funding will be allocated based on a comprehensive assessment of the potential impacts of climate change on developing countries.
2. Monitoring and evaluation: The proposed funding will be allocated to support monitoring and evaluation activities. The proposed funding will be allocated based on the feasibility and sustainability of the project.
3. Administrative costs: The proposed funding will be allocated to support administrative costs. The proposed funding will be allocated based on the potential for scalability and replicability.
4. Contingency funds: The proposed funding will be allocated to support contingency funds. The proposed funding will be allocated based on the potential for scalability and replicability.

## 7. Monitoring and Evaluation

The monitoring and evaluation strategy for the Green Climate Funding Proposal V 3.0 involves the following components:

1. Project implementation: The proposed funding will be allocated to support project implementation activities. The proposed funding will be allocated based on a comprehensive assessment of the potential impacts of climate change on developing countries.
2. Monitoring and evaluation: The proposed funding will be allocated to support monitoring and evaluation activities. The proposed funding will be allocated based on the feasibility and sustainability of the project.
3. Administrative costs: The proposed funding will be allocated to support administrative costs. The proposed funding will be allocated based on the potential for scalability and replicability.
4. Contingency funds: The proposed funding will be allocated to support contingency funds. The proposed funding will be allocated based on the potential for scalability and replicability.

## 8. Conclusion

The Green Climate Funding Proposal V 3.0 is a comprehensive document outlining the proposed funding for climate change mitigation and adaptation projects in developing countries. The proposed funding will be allocated to support projects that align with the project's objectives and scope, enhance resilience to climate change impacts, and promote sustainable development. The proposed funding will be allocated based on a comprehensive assessment of the potential impacts of climate change on developing countries.

## 9. Appendices

The appendices for the Green Climate Funding Proposal V 3.0 include:

1. Project proposal templates.
2. Budget and financial projection tables.
3. Risk management strategies.
4. Monitoring and evaluation plans.
5. Administrative cost estimates.
6. Contingency fund estimates.

## Date: [Date]

## Subject: Funding proposal for the Green Climate Finance Facility for Financing Climate Smart Agriculture in Senegal

Dear Mr. Diarte,

We refer to the project titled "Green Climate Finance Facility for Financing Climate Smart Agriculture in Senegal" (the "Project") and submit this proposal for your consideration. The Project aims to mobilize financial resources from various sources to support climate-smart agricultural practices in Senegal, with a focus on improving the resilience of farmers to climate change impacts.

## Background
Senegal is one of the most vulnerable countries to climate change, with significant risks to its agricultural sector. The country has been experiencing increased temperatures, altered rainfall patterns, and more frequent extreme weather events, which have led to reduced crop yields and livestock mortality. To address these challenges, Senegal is implementing the Green Climate Finance Facility (GCF) as part of its efforts to promote sustainable agriculture and reduce greenhouse gas emissions from the sector.

## Project Objectives
The primary objectives of the Project are:

1. To mobilize financial resources for climate-smart agricultural practices in Senegal, with a focus on improving the resilience of farmers to climate change impacts.
2. To support the development and implementation of climate-resilient agricultural technologies and practices, including irrigation management systems, crop diversification, and agroforestry.
3. To enhance the capacity of Senegalese farmers and other stakeholders to adapt to climate change impacts and improve their livelihoods through sustainable agriculture practices.
4. To promote the use of renewable energy sources in agricultural settings, including solar power and biogas production.
5. To support the development of early warning systems for extreme weather events and other climate-related hazards.

## Project Activities
The Project will involve the following activities:

1. Needs assessment and impact analysis to identify priority areas for climate-smart agricultural practices in Senegal.
2. Development of a national roadmap for climate-resilient agriculture, including policy recommendations and guidelines for farmers and other stakeholders.
3. Capacity building programs for farmers and other stakeholders to enhance their knowledge and skills in climate-smart agricultural practices.
4. Development of financial products and services tailored to the needs of Senegalese farmers, including insurance schemes and microfinance initiatives.
5. Implementation of climate-resilient agricultural technologies and practices, including irrigation management systems, crop diversification, and agroforestry.
6. Promotion of renewable energy sources in agricultural settings, including solar power and biogas production.
7. Development of early warning systems for extreme weather events and other climate-related hazards.
8. Monitoring and evaluation of the Project's progress and impact on climate change adaptation and mitigation in Senegal.

## Funding Requirements
The total funding required for the Project is [amount] USD, which will be provided by a combination of government grants, private sector investments, and international financial institutions. The funding will be used to support the implementation of the Project's activities and to ensure its long-term sustainability.

## Conclusion
We believe that the Green Climate Finance Facility for Financing Climate Smart Agriculture in Senegal is an important initiative to address the challenges posed by climate change to the country's agricultural sector. We are confident that with your support, we can mobilize financial resources and expertise to achieve our project objectives and contribute to the development of a more resilient and sustainable Senegal.

We look forward to the opportunity to discuss this proposal further and to explore ways in which we can work together to promote climate-smart agriculture in Senegal.

Sincerely,

[Your Name]
Minister of Environment and Transition Ecology
REPUBLICQUE DU SENEGAL

2. The document also highlights the importance of the Green Climate Fund's screening process, which involves assessing potential projects based on their alignment with the fund's objectives and criteria. The screening process is designed to ensure that only high-quality projects are selected for funding.
3. The document further discusses the role of the Green Climate Fund's Technical Advisory Panel (TAP) in providing technical assistance to project applicants. The TAP consists of experts from various fields, including climate science, economics, and development. The panel provides guidance on project design, implementation, and monitoring.
4. The document also mentions the importance of the Green Climate Fund's impact assessment process, which involves evaluating the potential social, environmental, and economic impacts of a project before it is funded. The impact assessment process helps to ensure that projects are aligned with the fund's objectives and do not have unintended negative consequences.
5. Finally, the document highlights the importance of the Green Climate Fund's transparency and accountability mechanisms, which include regular reporting on project progress and outcomes. These mechanisms help to ensure that the fund is being used effectively and efficiently to achieve its objectives.

## 1.6 Country Ownership

The GCFP objectives are fully aligned with Senegal's NDC and the relevant Senegalese national policies, including the National Agricultural Development Programme (PNAD) and the National Water Resources Management Plan (PNRWMP). The GCFP is designed to capitalize on Senegal's existing policy framework, benefiting from and contributing to ongoing national initiatives. The funding proposal is directly aligned with the priorities identified within these frameworks, by leveraging these established policies as a basis for the development of integrated water resource management (e.g., drip irrigation) and the dissemination of climate information.

## 1.7 The TAP acknowledges that the funding proposal was developed following extensive consultations with stakeholders, including the Government of Senegal, LHA, one of Senegal's two direct access entities, is leading the programme and has a strong track record in the sector. Rabah Microfinance Senegal, the co-executing entity, complements this effort through its expertise in commercialization of harvests.

## 1.8 The financial and resource contributions from the Government of Senegal and Rabah Microfinance Senegal are noteworthy from a country perspective, as they reflect the alignment of the funding proposal with local priorities.

## 1.9 Overall, the level of country ownership for this funding proposal is assessed to be high, reflecting the alignment of the funding proposal with national priorities and the involvement of key stakeholders in its development.

## Impact potential

Thank you for highlighting these critical aspects of the GCF. We appreciate the opportunity to address them and provide clarity on our approach.

We will ensure that all funded interventions adhere to robust climate adaptation principles. To this end, we will refine the eligibility criteria to emphasize demonstrable adaptation benefits tied directly to climate change impacts. We will also strengthen verification processes to validate reported outcomes, including independent reviews as needed.

## Scope of Eligible Technologies and Practices

The broad scope of eligible technologies is intended to address diverse climate risks, and we acknowledge the importance of evaluating and managing these risks in our adaptation strategies. We will prioritize technologies and practices that demonstrate measurable impacts on climate change mitigation and adaptation.

## Monitoring and Evaluation of Adaptation Outputs and Outcomes

LBA has in place a comprehensive MRV Protocol including a component on MRE to adapt to changing conditions and ensure effective implementation. We will prioritize technologies and practices that demonstrate measurable impacts on climate change mitigation and adaptation, and we will strengthen verification processes to validate reported outcomes, including independent reviews as needed.

## Conclusion

We appreciate the opportunity to address these critical aspects of the GCF. We appreciate the opportunity to address these critical aspects of the GCF.

## Country ownership

The All welcomes this assessment of Country Ownership of the program as High and thanks TAP for the comprehensive assessment

## Efficiency and effectiveness

Thanks you for your thoughtful observations regarding the internal rate of return (IRR), the use of concessional benefits, and other key aspects of the proposal. We recognize the importance of these factors in determining the financial sustainability with transformative impact. However, we acknowledge the concern that more efficient and differentiated pricing approaches may be needed to attract medium-sized farmers. The IFAP recommends a review of the rates or extended terms for loans targeting small and medium-sized farmers, especially those in developing countries.

## Potential Refinancing of Existing Portfolios

We appreciate TAP’s concerns about the potential for refinancing diluting the transformative ambition of the programme. IBA’s objective is to drive new investments in GSA. This will be achieved by:

* An explicit exclusion of refinancing will be incorporated into the project’s operating guidelines to ensure that GCP proceeds are strictly used for new GSA-focused projects and not for other purposes.
* The GCP Proceeds shall not finance any expenditures incurred prior to the effectiveness of the IFAP.
* Alternative pricing mechanisms will be established/track the use of GCF funds and ensure alignment with the programme’s objectives.

## Accredited Entity (AE) Fee

The AE fee of 4.57% reflects the administrative and operational efforts required to implement the initiative undertaken by the IFAP for the Highb

<doc> Gender Action Plan 
 Introduction 
 This Gender Action Plan has been developed along with a Gender Asse- 
 Agriculture Programme. 
 Objective: 
 The primary goal of this program is to significantly enhance farm prod- 
 uction as well as bolstering resilience against various climate-related challenges, 
 such as drought, pests, shortened seasons, and erratic weather patterns, 
 with the country's Nationally Determined Contributions (NDC) commi- 
 tment. 
 The specific objective is to ensure inclusion of gender issues in the agri- 
 cultural sector and strengthen the voices, the contribution, and the urgency 
 of people in climate smart actions in Senegal. The monitoring of the progress 
 and results will be done using the key performance indicators tools that will be reviewed from time to time over the project 
 period. 
 Key Components: 
 1. Innovative Financing Facility to provide concessional loans to foster climate-smart agricultural practices. This facility will provide concessional 
 loans to encourage the adoption of climate-smart agricultural practices among MSMEs, cooperatives, and farmer organizations. It will cover a range 
 of activities in crop, forestry, and livestock production, as well as energy 
 2. Capacity Building and Technical Assistance for LBA, FOs, women and youth organizations, cooperatives and MSMEs; this component is designed to increase 
 agricultural lending 
 3. Enabling environment for CSA practice adoption enhanced. This promoting climate-smart green finance mechanisms, it involves stake- CSA practices. It will also include provision of agricultural extension and </doc>


---
**Page 164**
---

<doc>     Dissemination of CSA Information, Knowledge Sharing, and Documentation: A comprehensive communication strategy will be implemented 
     to engage key stakeholders and program beneficiaries. This includes the development of promotional materials, a program website, and 
     efficient coordination, monitoring, and evaluation of program activities. 
     Program Management: A dedicated Program Management Unit (PMU) with a representative from gender office as part of the PMU will ensure 
     that the project is implemented in accordance with the agreed-upon objectives and budget. The GAP will be updated consistently as needed to ensure the 
     development of financial mechanisms to overcome market barriers, and sustaining loan portfolio, influencing market rates and promoting green 
     financing for the program. 
     Outcomes and Impact: 
     The program is expected to significantly reduce GHG emissions (an esti- 
     mated 3.76 million tCO2e over 20 years) and directly benefit approximately 
     152,266 people in Senegal of which 30 - 40% will be women. It will conti- 
     nue to promote a sustainable and inclusive economic growth model. 
     Sustainability and Replicability: 
     Sustainability will be ensured through the technical assistance component, 
     increased capacity of beneficiaries. The program aims to create a self-sustaining team portfolio, influencing market rates and promoting green 
     financing for the target farmers. 
     Implementation: A comprehensive communication strategy will be implemented 
     (i) with a representative from gender office as part of the PMU will ensure 
     that development of promotional materials, a program website, and 
     undertaking of baseline surveys and GCFF Annual Progress Reports, the GAP will be updated consistently as needed to ensure the

 | Outcome 14: Establish a dedicated Line of Credit to facilitate LBA’s GSA lending activities, with a focus on women-led MSMEs. Finally, the programme will also support the development of a dedicated Line of Credit focused on Climate Smart Agriculture |  |  |  |  |  |
